Typically you will need to update ``completed`` every time you have completed a step. You can do this by setting ``completed`` directly or by setting ``advance`` which will add to the current ``completed`` value.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:91:The :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.update` method collects keyword arguments which are also associated with the task. The additional arguments are stored in ``task.fields`` and may be referenced in :ref:`Column classes<Columns>`.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:96:You can show or hide tasks by updating the tasks ``visible`` value. You can also make the progress display disappear on exit by setting ``transient=True`` on the Progress constructor. Here's an example::\ndocs/source/progress.rst:104:    with Progress(transient=True) as progress:\ndocs/source/progress.rst:108:Transient progress displays are useful if you want more minimal output in the terminal when tasks are complete.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:113:When you add a task it is automatically *started*, which means it will show a progress bar at 0% and the time remaining will be calculated from the current time. This may not work well if there is a long delay before you can start updating progress; you may need to wait for a response from a server or count files in a directory (for example). In these cases you can call :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.add_task` with ``start=False`` or ``total=None`` which will display a pulsing animation that lets the user know something is working. This is known as an *indeterminate* progress bar. When you have the number of steps you can call :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.start_task` which will display the progress bar at 0%, then :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.update` as normal.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:118:By default, the progress information will refresh 10 times a second. You can set the refresh rate with the ``refresh_per_second`` argument on the :class:`~rich.progress.Progress` constructor. You should set this to something lower than 10 if you know your updates will not be that frequent.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:120:You might want to disable auto-refresh entirely if your updates are not very frequent, which you can do by setting ``auto_refresh=False`` on the constructor. If you disable auto-refresh you will need to call :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.refresh` manually after updating your task(s).\ndocs/source/progress.rst:126:The progress bar(s) will use only as much of the width of the terminal as required to show the task information. If you set the ``expand`` argument on the Progress constructor, then Rich will stretch the progress display to the full available width.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:132:You may customize the columns in the progress display with the positional arguments to the :class:`~rich.progress.Progress` constructor. The columns are specified as either a `format string <https://docs.python.org/3/library/string.html#formatspec>`_ or a :class:`~rich.progress.ProgressColumn` object.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:134:Format strings will be rendered with a single value `\"task\"` which will be a :class:`~rich.progress.Task` instance. For example ``\"{task.description}\"`` would display the task description in the column, and ``\"{task.completed} of {task.total}\"`` would display how many of the total steps have been completed. Additional fields passed via keyword arguments to `~rich.progress.Progress.update` are stored in ``task.fields``. You can add them to a format string with the following syntax: ``\"extra info: {task.fields[extra]}\"``.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:138:    progress = Progress(\ndocs/source/progress.rst:141:        TaskProgressColumn(),\ndocs/source/progress.rst:145:To create a Progress with your own columns in addition to the defaults, use :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.get_default_columns`::\ndocs/source/progress.rst:147:    progress = Progress(\ndocs/source/progress.rst:149:        *Progress.get_default_columns(),\ndocs/source/progress.rst:167:To implement your own columns, extend the :class:`~rich.progress.ProgressColumn` class and use it as you would the other columns.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:173:Rich builds a :class:`~rich.table.Table` for the tasks in the Progress instance. You can customize how the columns of this *tasks table* are created by specifying the ``table_column`` argument in the Column constructor, which should be a :class:`~rich.table.Column` instance.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:175:The following example demonstrates a progress bar where the description takes one third of the width of the terminal, and the bar takes up the remaining two thirds::\ndocs/source/progress.rst:180:    from rich.progress import Progress, BarColumn, TextColumn\ndocs/source/progress.rst:184:    progress = Progress(text_column, bar_column, expand=True)\ndocs/source/progress.rst:195:The Progress class will create an internal Console object which you can access via ``progress.console``. If you print or log to this console, the output will be displayed *above* the progress display. Here's an example::\ndocs/source/progress.rst:197:    with Progress() as progress:\ndocs/source/progress.rst:204:If you have another Console object you want to use, pass it in to the :class:`~rich.progress.Progress` constructor. Here's an example::\ndocs/source/progress.rst:208:    with Progress(console=my_console) as progress:\ndocs/source/progress.rst:216:To avoid breaking the progress display visuals, Rich will redirect ``stdout`` and ``stderr`` so that you can use the built-in ``print`` statement. This feature is enabled by default, but you can disable by setting ``redirect_stdout`` or ``redirect_stderr`` to ``False``\ndocs/source/progress.rst:222:If the :class:`~rich.progress.Progress` class doesn't offer exactly what you need in terms of a progress display, you can override the :class:`~rich.progress.Progress.get_renderables` method. For example, the following class will render a :class:`~rich.panel.Panel` around the progress display::\ndocs/source/progress.rst:225:    from rich.progress import Progress\ndocs/source/progress.rst:227:    class MyProgress(Progress):\ndocs/source/progress.rst:263:If you expect to be reading from multiple files, you can use :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.open` or :meth:`~rich.progress.Progress.wrap_file` to add a file progress to an existing Progress instance.\ndocs/source/progress.rst:268:Nesting Progress bars\ndocs/source/progress.rst:290:Multiple Progress\ndocs/source/progress.rst:293:You can't have different columns per task with a single Progress instance. However, you can have as many Progress instances as you like in a :ref:`live`. See `live_progress.py <https://github.com/willmcgugan/rich/blob/master/examples/live_progress.py>`_ and `dynamic_progress.py <https://github.com/willmcgugan/rich/blob/master/examples/dynamic_progress.py>`_ for examples of using multiple Progress instances.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}
